user
You are the second reviewer on a routine security code review of our own
nix-bitcoin fork: the NixOS modules and packaging we deploy on our own Bitcoin
and Lightning node. Two prior reviewers have already produced the findings
below against the exact commit checked out in the current directory. Your job
is quality control on their work, as the maintainer's independent check:

For EACH finding, open the cited file at the cited location and decide:
- verdict: "confirmed" (the defect is real as described), "false-positive"
  (the code does not do what the finding claims, or an existing control
  already prevents it — name the control), or "needs-info" (cannot be decided
  from the tree; say what is missing).
- severity: your own call (critical/high/medium/low/info), which may differ
  from the reviewer's.
- reasoning: one to three sentences grounded in the code you read (quote the
  line or construct that decides it).
- fix_ok: whether the reviewer's recommended fix is correct and complete
  (true/false) and, if false, what is wrong with it.

Then, only for the files the findings touch, note up to three defects the
reviewers missed or misread that a maintainer should know about ("missed").
Do not pad: if there is nothing, say so.

Read-only tools only; do not modify the tree. Be terse and concrete.

End your answer with exactly one fenced ```json block of this shape:
{"reviews":[{"id":"<finding id or title>","verdict":"confirmed|false-positive|needs-info","severity":"...","reasoning":"...","fix_ok":true,"fix_note":""}],
 "missed":[{"file":"...","line":0,"severity":"...","title":"...","description":"..."}],
 "summary":"two or three sentences"}
followed by nothing else.

.github/workflows/test.yml:16:
.github/workflows/test.yml:17:# Strategy:
.github/workflows/test.yml:18:# Job `build_test_drivers` evals all VM tests and builds all test drivers in a single Nix build.
.github/workflows/test.yml:19:# Compared to launching a separate build job for each VM test, this avoids duplicated
.github/workflows/test.yml:20:# builds of derivations that the test drivers have in common.
.github/workflows/test.yml:21:#
.github/workflows/test.yml:22:# The job matrix `test_scenario` runs the VM tests. Scenario jobs ALWAYS run —
.github/workflows/test.yml:23:# build-to-cachix.sh early-exits green when a scenario's result is already in
.github/workflows/test.yml:24:# the binary cache — so every push reports a real conclusion for all five
.github/workflows/test.yml:25:# checks. (A job-level skip leaves the matrix contexts unreported, and the
.github/workflows/test.yml:26:# release branch ruleset then blocks fast-forwards forever with
.github/workflows/test.yml:27:# "required status checks are expected".)

helper/update-flake.sh:1:#!/usr/bin/env bash
helper/update-flake.sh:2:set -euo pipefail
helper/update-flake.sh:3:
helper/update-flake.sh:4:# This script does the following:
helper/update-flake.sh:5:# - When called without arguments, update all flake inputs, including nixpkgs.
helper/update-flake.sh:6:# - When called with a version argument, set input `nixpkgs` in `flake.nix` to the
helper/update-flake.sh:7:#   specified version and only update this input.
helper/update-flake.sh:8:# - Print version updates of pinned pkgs like so:
helper/update-flake.sh:9:#     Pkg updates in nixpkgs unstable:
helper/update-flake.sh:10:#     bitcoin: 0.20.0 -> 0.21.1
helper/update-flake.sh:11:#     btcpayserver: 1.1.0 -> 1.1.2
helper/update-flake.sh:12:# - Write ../pkgs/pinned.nix:
helper/update-flake.sh:13:#     Packages for which the stable und unstable versions are identical are
helper/update-flake.sh:14:#     pinned to stable.
helper/update-flake.sh:15:#     All other pkgs are pinned to unstable.
helper/update-flake.sh:16:
helper/update-flake.sh:17:forceRun=
helper/update-flake.sh:18:nixosVersion=
helper/update-flake.sh:19:for arg in "$@"; do
helper/update-flake.sh:20:    case $arg in
helper/update-flake.sh:21:        -f)
helper/update-flake.sh:22:            forceRun=1
helper/update-flake.sh:23:            ;;
helper/update-flake.sh:24:        *)
helper/update-flake.sh:25:            nixosVersion=$arg
helper/update-flake.sh:26:            ;;
helper/update-flake.sh:27:    esac
helper/update-flake.sh:28:done
helper/update-flake.sh:29:
helper/update-flake.sh:30:# cd to script dir
helper/update-flake.sh:31:cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}"
helper/update-flake.sh:32:
helper/update-flake.sh:33:if [[ $(nix flake 2>&1) != *"requires a sub-command"* ]]; then
helper/update-flake.sh:34:    echo "Error. This script requires nix flake support."
helper/update-flake.sh:35:    echo "https://nixos.wiki/wiki/Flakes#Installing_flakes"
helper/update-flake.sh:36:    exit 1
helper/update-flake.sh:37:fi
helper/update-flake.sh:38:
helper/update-flake.sh:39:if [[ ! $forceRun ]] && ! git diff --quiet ../flake.{nix,lock}; then
helper/update-flake.sh:40:    echo "error: flake.nix/flake.lock have changes. Run with option -f to ignore."
helper/update-flake.sh:41:    exit 1
helper/update-flake.sh:42:fi
helper/update-flake.sh:43:
helper/update-flake.sh:44:if [[ ! $nixosVersion ]]; then
helper/update-flake.sh:45:    echo "Updating flake 'nixos-search'"
helper/update-flake.sh:46:    nix flake update --flake ../test/nixos-search
helper/update-flake.sh:47:    echo
helper/update-flake.sh:48:fi
helper/update-flake.sh:49:
helper/update-flake.sh:50:versions=$(nix eval --json -f update-flake.nix versions)
helper/update-flake.sh:51:
helper/update-flake.sh:52:## Uncomment the following to generate a version change message for testing
helper/update-flake.sh:53:# versions=$(echo "$versions" | sed 's|1|0|g')
helper/update-flake.sh:54:
helper/update-flake.sh:55:setVersion() {
helper/update-flake.sh:56:    sed -Ei "s|($1)[0-9.]+|\1$nixosVersion|" "$2"
helper/update-flake.sh:57:}
helper/update-flake.sh:58:
helper/update-flake.sh:59:echo "Updating main flake"
helper/update-flake.sh:60:if [[ $nixosVersion ]]; then

helper/update-flake.sh:65:    setVersion 'update-flake.sh ' ../dev/README.md
helper/update-flake.sh:66:    nix flake update nixpkgs --flake ..
helper/update-flake.sh:67:else
helper/update-flake.sh:68:    nix flake update --flake ..
helper/update-flake.sh:69:fi
helper/update-flake.sh:70:
helper/update-flake.sh:71:echo
helper/update-flake.sh:72:nix eval --raw -f update-flake.nix --argstr prevVersions "$versions" showUpdates; echo
helper/update-flake.sh:73:
helper/update-flake.sh:74:pinned=../pkgs/pinned.nix
helper/update-flake.sh:75:pinnedSrc=$(nix eval --raw -f update-flake.nix --argstr prevVersions "$versions" pinnedFile)
helper/update-flake.sh:76:if [[ $pinnedSrc != $(cat "$pinned") ]]; then

helper/update-flake.sh:78:    echo
helper/update-flake.sh:79:    echo "Updated pinned.nix"
helper/update-flake.sh:80:fi

modules/netns-isolation.nix:184:          ${iptables} -w -A INPUT -s ${allowedAddresses} -j ACCEPT
modules/netns-isolation.nix:185:        '' + optionalString (config.services.${n}.tor.enforce or false) ''
modules/netns-isolation.nix:186:          ${iptables} -w -P OUTPUT DROP
modules/netns-isolation.nix:187:          ${iptables} -w -A OUTPUT -d ${allowedAddresses} -j ACCEPT
modules/netns-isolation.nix:188:        '';
modules/netns-isolation.nix:189:        script = name: src: pkgs.writers.writeDash name ''
modules/netns-isolation.nix:190:          set -e
modules/netns-isolation.nix:191:          ${src}
modules/netns-isolation.nix:192:        '';
modules/netns-isolation.nix:193:      in {
modules/netns-isolation.nix:194:        "${n}".serviceConfig.NetworkNamespacePath = "/var/run/netns/${netnsName}";
modules/netns-isolation.nix:195:
modules/netns-isolation.nix:196:        "netns-${n}" = rec {
modules/netns-isolation.nix:197:          requires = [ "nb-netns-bridge.service" ];
modules/netns-isolation.nix:198:          after = [ "nb-netns-bridge.service" ];
modules/netns-isolation.nix:199:          requiredBy = [ "${n}.service" ];
modules/netns-isolation.nix:200:          before = requiredBy;
modules/netns-isolation.nix:201:          serviceConfig = {
modules/netns-isolation.nix:202:            Type = "oneshot";
modules/netns-isolation.nix:203:            RemainAfterExit = true;
modules/netns-isolation.nix:204:            ExecStart = script "setup" setup;
modules/netns-isolation.nix:205:          };
modules/netns-isolation.nix:206:          # Link deletion is implicit in netns deletion, but it sometimes only happens
modules/netns-isolation.nix:207:          # after `netns delete` finishes. Add an extra `link del` to ensure that
modules/netns-isolation.nix:208:          # the link is deleted before the service stops, which is needed for service
modules/netns-isolation.nix:209:          # restart to succeed.
modules/netns-isolation.nix:210:          preStop = ''
modules/netns-isolation.nix:211:            ${ip} netns delete ${netnsName}
modules/netns-isolation.nix:212:            ${ip} link del ${peer} 2> /dev/null || true
modules/netns-isolation.nix:213:          '';
modules/netns-isolation.nix:214:
modules/netns-isolation.nix:215:        };
modules/netns-isolation.nix:216:      };
modules/netns-isolation.nix:217:    in
modules/netns-isolation.nix:218:      foldl (services: n:
modules/netns-isolation.nix:219:        services // (makeNetnsServices n netns.${n})
modules/netns-isolation.nix:220:      ) {} (builtins.attrNames netns)
modules/netns-isolation.nix:221:    );
modules/netns-isolation.nix:222:  }

modules/btcpayserver.nix:117:        # over-grants for the RPC user reachable from the internet-facing
modules/btcpayserver.nix:118:        # btcpayserver: setban lets a web-app compromise eclipse the node
modules/btcpayserver.nix:119:        # (persistent peer bans), and generatetoaddress burns CPU via `maxtries`

modules/btcpayserver.nix:191:        # reload-on-change watcher registered an inotify watch on every

modules/btcpayserver.nix:194:        # exhausted the per-user inotify watch limit within days and systemd

docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:6:that the wireguard preset sets `services.lnd.restAddress = "0.0.0.0"`
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:7:(modules/presets/wireguard.nix), exposing the macaroon-admin REST API on all
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:8:interfaces and relying solely on the nixos-fw firewall (default-drop plus an
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:9:accept rule for the wg subnet) for protection. Severity medium/low, advisory.
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:10:The suggested fix — bind to the wg server address (`${subnet}.1`, default
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:11:10.10.0.1) instead — is correct as defense-in-depth but **breaks several
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:12:things that are non-obvious**, because of one load-bearing detail:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:13:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:14:> `nbLib.address` (pkgs/lib.nix) maps the bind address `0.0.0.0` to the
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:15:> *connect* address `127.0.0.1`. Every internal consumer of `restAddress`
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:16:> goes through it. Today they all silently talk to localhost, which the
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:17:> default TLS cert covers. Bind to `10.10.0.1` and they **all** follow.
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:18:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:19:## What a naive `restAddress = serverAddress` breaks
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:20:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:21:1. **lnd startup becomes hard-coupled to WireGuard.** lnd fails fatally if it
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:22:   cannot bind `restlisten`; the `10.10.0.1` address only exists once
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:23:   `wireguard-wg-nb.service` has configured the interface. Without systemd
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:24:   ordering, boot is a crash-loop race (`Restart=on-failure`/10s masks it
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:25:   until it doesn't). *With* ordering, a broken wg setup (e.g. missing
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:26:   `wg-server-private-key` secret) takes the whole lightning node down —
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:27:   today lnd runs fine with wg broken. This coupling is inherent to the fix;
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:28:   it must be a conscious acceptance, or defused with
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:29:   `net.ipv4.ip_nonlocal_bind = 1` (system-wide sysctl loosening — an
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:30:   auditor trade-off of its own).
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:31:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:32:2. **TLS cert SANs.** The generated lnd cert only has
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:33:   `DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1` plus `rpcAddress` when non-local
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:34:   (modules/secrets/secrets.nix `doMakeCert`; modules/lnd.nix
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:35:   `certificate.extraIPs`). Nothing adds the REST address. Any validating
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:36:   client connecting to `https://10.10.0.1:8080` fails TLS. The preset must
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:37:   set `services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = [ serverAddress ]`. Note
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:38:   `makeCert` *does* regenerate the cert when alt-names change
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:39:   (secrets.nix, `$name-cert-alt-names` comparison), so deployed nodes pick
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:40:   the new SAN up on the next deploy — but anything that pinned the old cert
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:41:   must tolerate rotation.
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:42:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:43:3. **Macaroon creation runs in lnd's `ExecStartPost`** (modules/lnd.nix): a
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:44:   root script curls `https://<nbLib.address restAddress>:<restPort>/v1` with
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:45:   `--cacert`. It would move from 127.0.0.1 to 10.10.0.1 → fails without
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:46:   item 2 and item 1 → **lnd.service itself fails post-start**, even though
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:47:   the daemon is healthy.
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:48:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:49:4. **btcpayserver's lnd-rest backend** (modules/btcpayserver.nix,
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:50:   `btclightning=...server=https://<address restAddress>...`) moves to
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:51:   10.10.0.1 too: needs the SAN fix and makes btcpayserver transitively
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:52:   wg-dependent.
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:53:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:54:5. **lndconnect onion mode** (modules/lndconnect.nix): the Tor hidden
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:55:   service forwards to `nbLib.address lnd.restAddress`. Tor-based mobile
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:56:   access would then depend on the wg interface being up.
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:57:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:58:6. **nodeinfo** (modules/nodeinfo.nix) reports `rest_address` — cosmetic,
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:59:   but scripts parsing it see a new value.
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:60:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:61:## Recommended design (avoids 2–6 entirely)
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:62:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:63:Do **not** touch `restAddress`. Leave it at its default `127.0.0.1` and have
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:64:the preset add a *second* listener — lnd accepts repeated `restlisten`
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:65:entries:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:66:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:67:```nix
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:68:services.lnd.extraConfig = ''
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:69:  restlisten=${serverAddress}:${toString lnd.restPort}
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:70:'';
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:71:```
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:72:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:73:All internal consumers keep localhost (valid cert SAN, no wg coupling); the
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:74:wg peer reaches `10.10.0.1:8080` exactly as before (`lndconnect-wg` already
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:75:targets `serverAddress` with `--nocert`). Only item 1 — the bind-time
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:76:dependency on the interface — remains, and still needs:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:77:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:78:- `systemd.services.lnd = { wants = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; after = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; }`
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:79:  in the preset (runtime wg restarts are fine: a bound socket survives
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:80:  interface teardown and resumes when the address returns);
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:81:- the firewall accept rule stays (nixos-fw default-drops; the rule is what
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:82:  admits the peer). Since 2026-09-09 it is already scoped to the interface
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:83:  and the single peer (`-i wg-nb -s <peerAddress>/32`), closing the
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:84:  2026-08-29 low: a spoofed-source packet on the external interface no
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:85:  longer matches the rule at all, independent of rp_filter/routing.
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:86:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:87:## Pre-flight checklist before shipping any variant
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:88:
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:89:- [ ] Decide on the lnd↔wg startup coupling (accept, or `ip_nonlocal_bind`).
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:90:- [ ] `test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix`: assert lnd comes up cleanly from a
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:91:      cold boot (ordering race), REST reachable over wg at
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:92:      `serverAddress:restPort`, REST **not** bound on the external
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:93:      interface (the actual point of the fix), macaroon `ExecStartPost`
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:94:      succeeded, and — if btcpayserver is in the scenario — its lnd backend
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:95:      connects.
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:96:- [ ] Full local VM suite green (`./test/run-tests.sh`).
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:97:- [ ] Verify on a staging deploy that cert regeneration (if `restAddress`
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:98:      was changed rather than the extraConfig variant) does not strand
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:99:      existing clients.
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:100:- [ ] Re-run the adversarial LLM audit (audits branch) — per policy every
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:101:      fix re-runs the whole review; the review should specifically re-check
docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md:102:      items 1–6.

modules/bitcoind.nix:391:      wants = [
modules/bitcoind.nix:392:        "network-online.target"
modules/bitcoind.nix:393:        # Use `wants` instead of `requires` for `nix-bitcoin-secrets.target`
modules/bitcoind.nix:394:        # so that bitcoind and all dependent services are not restarted when
modules/bitcoind.nix:395:        # the secrets target restarts.
modules/bitcoind.nix:396:        # The secrets target always restarts when deploying with one of the methods
modules/bitcoind.nix:397:        # in ./deployment.
modules/bitcoind.nix:398:        #
modules/bitcoind.nix:399:        # TODO-EXTERNAL: Instead of `wants`, use a future systemd dependency type

modules/bitcoind.nix:402:      ];

modules/bitcoind.nix:430:          install -o '${cfg.user}' -g '${cfg.group}' -m 640 <(echo "$cfg") $confFile
modules/bitcoind.nix:431:        fi
modules/bitcoind.nix:432:      '';
modules/bitcoind.nix:433:
modules/bitcoind.nix:434:      # Enable RPC access for group
modules/bitcoind.nix:435:      postStart = ''
modules/bitcoind.nix:436:        # NOTE: this makes the RPC cookie readable by group `bitcoin` — every
modules/bitcoind.nix:437:        # service in that group gets full privileged RPC. Keep group membership
modules/bitcoind.nix:438:        # minimal; new services should use an rpc user + HMAC instead (audit L-10).
modules/bitcoind.nix:439:        # Use an explicit 0640 rather than `chmod g=r`: the latter leaves the
modules/bitcoind.nix:440:        # other-read bit at whatever bitcoind created (its umask), so the cookie's
modules/bitcoind.nix:441:        # world-readability would hinge on the app's file mode. 0640 clears it.
modules/bitcoind.nix:442:        chmod 0640 '${cfg.dataDir}/${optionalString cfg.regtest "regtest/"}.cookie'
modules/bitcoind.nix:443:      '' + (optionalString cfg.regtest) ''
modules/bitcoind.nix:444:        chmod g=x '${cfg.dataDir}/regtest'
modules/bitcoind.nix:445:      '';
modules/bitcoind.nix:446:

modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:1:# RPC calls that are safe for public use.
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:2:#
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:3:# Safety bar: bounded CPU cost per call, no P2P side effects beyond
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:4:# transaction relay, no leaks of peer topology or wallet contents.
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:5:#
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:6:# Deliberately excluded (audit M-3):
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:7:# - scantxoutset, gettxoutsetinfo, getblocktemplate: minutes-scale CPU,
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:8:#   trivially abused for DoS by any holder of the public credentials.
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:9:# - getblockfrompeer: lets the caller drive the node's P2P block fetching.
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:10:# - fundrawtransaction: operates on a loaded bitcoind wallet — it selects and
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:11:#   locks UTXOs and reveals change/wallet contents, violating the wallet-safety
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:12:#   bar above. No local consumer (lnd, electrs, nbxplorer) uses it; lnd/nbxplorer
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:13:#   manage their own wallets and electrs only reads blocks. Removed in the audit
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:14:#   follow-up 2026-08-20.
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:15:# If you need any of these, grant them via
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:16:# `services.bitcoind.rpc.users.public.rpcwhitelist` in your own config.
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:17:#
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:18:# NOTE: getpeerinfo / getnodeaddresses stay in the list. This "public" user
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:19:# is also the default RPC user that LOCAL services use, and lnd REQUIRES both
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:20:# (its chain-backend health check + pruned-mode block fetching) — removing
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:21:# them makes lnd fail its health check and shut itself down. They only leak
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:22:# peer topology if you actually expose this user over a public proxy; if you
modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix:23:# do that, narrow the whitelist in your own config rather than here.

   112	      enable = true;
   113	      rpc.users.btcpayserver = {
   114	        passwordHMACFromFile = true;
   115	        # nbxplorer/btcpayserver need only the read/relay calls in the public
   116	        # whitelist. The former `setban` + `generatetoaddress` extras were
   117	        # over-grants for the RPC user reachable from the internet-facing
   118	        # btcpayserver: setban lets a web-app compromise eclipse the node
   119	        # (persistent peer bans), and generatetoaddress burns CPU via `maxtries`
   120	        # on mainnet and is regtest-only otherwise. Neither is used by nbxplorer,
   121	        # btcpayserver, or the test suite. Removed in the audit follow-up
   122	        # 2026-08-20; if you run btcpay in regtest and want its "mine" button,
   123	        # grant generatetoaddress in your own config.
   124	        rpcwhitelist = cfg.bitcoind.rpc.users.public.rpcwhitelist;
   125	        # Note: nbxplorer's Indexer.ConnectNode needs getpeerinfo — it is in the
   126	        # public whitelist (which this inherits), because lnd needs it too. See
   127	        # modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix.
   128	      };
   129	      listenWhitelisted = true;
   130	    };

   170	      '';

   172	      wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
   173	      requires = [ "postgresql.target" ];
   174	      wants = [ "bitcoind.service" ];
   175	      after = requires ++ wants ++ [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   176	      environment = dotnetNoReload;
   177	      preStart = ''
   178	        install -m 600 ${configFile} '${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}/settings.config'
   179	        {
   180	          echo "btcrpcpassword=$(cat ${config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir}/bitcoin-rpcpassword-btcpayserver)"
   181	        } >> '${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}/settings.config'
   182	      '';
   183	      serviceConfig = nbLib.defaultHardening // {
   184	        ExecStart = ''
   185	          ${cfg.nbxplorer.package}/bin/nbxplorer --conf=${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}/settings.config \
   186	            --datadir=${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}
   187	        '';
   188	        User = cfg.nbxplorer.user;
   189	        # The .NET host takes its ASP.NET content root from the working
   190	        # directory. Without an explicit one that root was `/`, and the host's
   191	        # reload-on-change watcher registered an inotify watch on every
   192	        # directory of the root filesystem -- the whole Nix store -- plus one
   193	        # for each directory created afterwards. On a node that builds, this
   194	        # exhausted the per-user inotify watch limit within days and systemd
   195	        # then failed to set up cgroup accounting for every new unit.
   196	        # Use the immutable app directory (as btcpayserver does below), NOT the
   197	        # data directory: a writable content root would let anything running
   198	        # as the nbxplorer user drop an `appsettings.json` that overrides the
   199	        # Nix-managed settings.config on the next start.
   200	        WorkingDirectory = "${cfg.nbxplorer.package}/lib/nbxplorer";
   201	        Restart = "on-failure";
   202	        RestartSec = "10s";
   203	        ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir ];
   204	        # .NET's JIT needs writable+executable pages, so W^X enforcement cannot
   205	        # be enabled for NBXplorer or BTCPay. Accepted trade-off (audit
   206	        # 2026-08-29, low): the remaining sandbox (nbLib.defaultHardening,
   207	        # netns/Tor policy, dedicated users) is unchanged.
   208	        MemoryDenyWriteExecute = false;
   209	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.nbxplorer.tor.enforce;
   210	    };
   211

   215	      configFile = builtins.toFile "btcpayserver-config" (''

   243	            --datadir='${cfg.btcpayserver.dataDir}'
   244	        '';
   245	        User = cfg.btcpayserver.user;
   246	        # Since 2.4.0, btcpayserver uses `Host.CreateDefaultBuilder`, which sets the
   247	        # ASP.NET content root to the working directory instead of the app directory.
   248	        # The web root (`wwwroot`) is resolved relative to the content root, so

   255	        Restart = "always";

   258	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.btcpayserver.tor.enforce;
   259	      startLimitIntervalSec = 30;
   260	      startLimitBurst = 10;
   261	    };

/bin/bash -lc 'nl -ba docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md' in /home/dan/fork-audit/src-so-2026-10-01-114021
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# Deferred: bind lnd's REST API to the WireGuard address
     2	
     3	Status: **DEFERRED — do not implement without working through every item below.**
     4	
     5	The 2026-08-24 adversarial audit (run `2026-08-24-111031`, both models) flagged
     6	that the wireguard preset sets `services.lnd.restAddress = "0.0.0.0"`
     7	(modules/presets/wireguard.nix), exposing the macaroon-admin REST API on all
     8	interfaces and relying solely on the nixos-fw firewall (default-drop plus an
     9	accept rule for the wg subnet) for protection. Severity medium/low, advisory.
    10	The suggested fix — bind to the wg server address (`${subnet}.1`, default
    11	10.10.0.1) instead — is correct as defense-in-depth but **breaks several
    12	things that are non-obvious**, because of one load-bearing detail:
    13	
    14	> `nbLib.address` (pkgs/lib.nix) maps the bind address `0.0.0.0` to the
    15	> *connect* address `127.0.0.1`. Every internal consumer of `restAddress`
    16	> goes through it. Today they all silently talk to localhost, which the
    17	> default TLS cert covers. Bind to `10.10.0.1` and they **all** follow.
    18	
    19	## What a naive `restAddress = serverAddress` breaks
    20	
    21	1. **lnd startup becomes hard-coupled to WireGuard.** lnd fails fatally if it
    22	   cannot bind `restlisten`; the `10.10.0.1` address only exists once
    23	   `wireguard-wg-nb.service` has configured the interface. Without systemd
    24	   ordering, boot is a crash-loop race (`Restart=on-failure`/10s masks it
    25	   until it doesn't). *With* ordering, a broken wg setup (e.g. missing
    26	   `wg-server-private-key` secret) takes the whole lightning node down —
    27	   today lnd runs fine with wg broken. This coupling is inherent to the fix;
    28	   it must be a conscious acceptance, or defused with
    29	   `net.ipv4.ip_nonlocal_bind = 1` (system-wide sysctl loosening — an
    30	   auditor trade-off of its own).
    31	
    32	2. **TLS cert SANs.** The generated lnd cert only has
    33	   `DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1` plus `rpcAddress` when non-local
    34	   (modules/secrets/secrets.nix `doMakeCert`; modules/lnd.nix
    35	   `certificate.extraIPs`). Nothing adds the REST address. Any validating
    36	   client connecting to `https://10.10.0.1:8080` fails TLS. The preset must
    37	   set `services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = [ serverAddress ]`. Note
    38	   `makeCert` *does* regenerate the cert when alt-names change
    39	   (secrets.nix, `$name-cert-alt-names` comparison), so deployed nodes pick
    40	   the new SAN up on the next deploy — but anything that pinned the old cert
    41	   must tolerate rotation.
    42	
    43	3. **Macaroon creation runs in lnd's `ExecStartPost`** (modules/lnd.nix): a
    44	   root script curls `https://<nbLib.address restAddress>:<restPort>/v1` with
    45	   `--cacert`. It would move from 127.0.0.1 to 10.10.0.1 → fails without
    46	   item 2 and item 1 → **lnd.service itself fails post-start**, even though
    47	   the daemon is healthy.
    48	
    49	4. **btcpayserver's lnd-rest backend** (modules/btcpayserver.nix,
    50	   `btclightning=...server=https://<address restAddress>...`) moves to
    51	   10.10.0.1 too: needs the SAN fix and makes btcpayserver transitively
    52	   wg-dependent.
    53	
    54	5. **lndconnect onion mode** (modules/lndconnect.nix): the Tor hidden
    55	   service forwards to `nbLib.address lnd.restAddress`. Tor-based mobile
    56	   access would then depend on the wg interface being up.
    57	
    58	6. **nodeinfo** (modules/nodeinfo.nix) reports `rest_address` — cosmetic,
    59	   but scripts parsing it see a new value.
    60	
    61	## Recommended design (avoids 2–6 entirely)
    62	
    63	Do **not** touch `restAddress`. Leave it at its default `127.0.0.1` and have
    64	the preset add a *second* listener — lnd accepts repeated `restlisten`
    65	entries:
    66	
    67	```nix
    68	services.lnd.extraConfig = ''
    69	  restlisten=${serverAddress}:${toString lnd.restPort}
    70	'';
    71	```
    72	
    73	All internal consumers keep localhost (valid cert SAN, no wg coupling); the
    74	wg peer reaches `10.10.0.1:8080` exactly as before (`lndconnect-wg` already
    75	targets `serverAddress` with `--nocert`). Only item 1 — the bind-time
    76	dependency on the interface — remains, and still needs:
    77	
    78	- `systemd.services.lnd = { wants = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; after = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; }`
    79	  in the preset (runtime wg restarts are fine: a bound socket survives
    80	  interface teardown and resumes when the address returns);
    81	- the firewall accept rule stays (nixos-fw default-drops; the rule is what
    82	  admits the peer). Since 2026-09-09 it is already scoped to the interface
    83	  and the single peer (`-i wg-nb -s <peerAddress>/32`), closing the
    84	  2026-08-29 low: a spoofed-source packet on the external interface no
    85	  longer matches the rule at all, independent of rp_filter/routing.
    86	
    87	## Pre-flight checklist before shipping any variant
    88	
    89	- [ ] Decide on the lnd↔wg startup coupling (accept, or `ip_nonlocal_bind`).
    90	- [ ] `test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix`: assert lnd comes up cleanly from a
    91	      cold boot (ordering race), REST reachable over wg at
    92	      `serverAddress:restPort`, REST **not** bound on the external
    93	      interface (the actual point of the fix), macaroon `ExecStartPost`
    94	      succeeded, and — if btcpayserver is in the scenario — its lnd backend
    95	      connects.
    96	- [ ] Full local VM suite green (`./test/run-tests.sh`).
    97	- [ ] Verify on a staging deploy that cert regeneration (if `restAddress`
    98	      was changed rather than the extraConfig variant) does not strand
    99	      existing clients.
   100	- [ ] Re-run the adversarial LLM audit (audits branch) — per policy every
   101	      fix re-runs the whole review; the review should specifically re-check
   102	      items 1–6.

codex
The QEMU listener does bind all host interfaces and authorizes the demo key. The WireGuard preset has the reported firewall assertions; I’m checking LND authentication and local consumers to assess the remaining risk and the proposed listener change.

   193	  config = mkIf cfg.enable {
   194	    services.bitcoind = {
   195	      enable = true;
   196	
   197	      # Increase rpc thread count due to reports that lightning implementations fail
   198	      # under high bitcoind rpc load
   199	      rpc.threads = 16;
   200	
   201	      zmqpubrawblock = mkDefault "tcp://${bitcoindRpcAddress}:28332";
   202	      zmqpubrawtx = mkDefault "tcp://${bitcoindRpcAddress}:28333";
   203	    };
   204

   206

   208	      "d '${cfg.dataDir}' 0770 ${cfg.user} ${cfg.group} - -"
   209	    ];
   210	
   211	    services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = mkIf (cfg.rpcAddress != "127.0.0.1") [ "${cfg.rpcAddress}" ];
   212	
   213	    systemd.services.lnd = {
   214	      wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
   215	      # `wants`, not `requires`: lnd is built to tolerate its chain backend
   216	      # going away and reconnects on its own. A hard `requires` meant every
   217	      # bitcoind restart (e.g. any deploy that touches bitcoind config) STOPPED
   218	      # lnd, and `nixos-rebuild switch` did not restart it — leaving lnd (and
   219	      # Lightning) down until a manual start. `after` still orders lnd behind
   220	      # bitcoind at boot; `wants` pulls bitcoind in without the cascade-stop.
   221	      wants = [ "bitcoind.service" ];
   222	      after = [ "bitcoind.service" "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   223	      preStart = ''
   224	        install -m600 ${configFile} '${cfg.dataDir}/lnd.conf'
   225	        {
   226	          echo "bitcoind.rpcpass=$(cat ${secretsDir}/bitcoin-rpcpassword-public)"
   227	          ${optionalString (cfg.getPublicAddressCmd != "") ''
   228	            echo "externalip=$(${cfg.getPublicAddressCmd})"
   229	          ''}
   230	        } >> '${cfg.dataDir}/lnd.conf'
   231	
   232	        if [[ ! -f ${networkDir}/wallet.db ]]; then
   233	          seed='${cfg.dataDir}/lnd-seed-mnemonic'
   234	
   235	          if [[ ! -f "$seed" ]]; then
   236	            echo "Create lnd seed"
   237	            (umask u=r,go=; ${lndinit} gen-seed > "$seed")
   238	          fi
   239	
   240	          echo "Create lnd wallet"
   241	          ${lndinit} -v init-wallet \
   242	            --file.seed="$seed" \
   243	            --file.wallet-password='${secretsDir}/lnd-wallet-password' \
   244	            --init-file.output-wallet-dir='${cfg.networkDir}'
   245	        fi
   246	      '';
   247	      serviceConfig = nbLib.defaultHardening // {
   248	        Type = "notify";
   249	        RuntimeDirectory = "lnd"; # Only used to store custom macaroons
   250	        RuntimeDirectoryMode = "711";
   251	        ExecStart = "${cfg.package}/bin/lnd --configfile='${cfg.dataDir}/lnd.conf'";
   252	        User = cfg.user;
   253	        TimeoutSec = "15min";
   254	        Restart = "on-failure";
   255	        RestartSec = "10s";
   256	        ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.dataDir ];
   257	        ExecStartPost = let
   258	          curl = "${pkgs.curl}/bin/curl -fsS --cacert ${cfg.certPath}";
   259	          restUrl = "https://${nbLib.addressWithPort cfg.restAddress cfg.restPort}/v1";
   260	          # Setting macaroon permissions for other users needs root permissions
   261	          # The admin macaroon is passed to curl via a fd because argv is
   262	          # world-readable through /proc/<pid>/cmdline
   263	          script = nbLib.rootScript "lnd-create-macaroons" ''
   264	            umask ug=r,o=
   265	            # $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY (/run/lnd) is writable by the lnd service user,
   266	            # so this root script must never write/chmod/chown through a path the
   267	            # lnd process could tamper with (else a compromised lnd escalates to
   268	            # arbitrary root file overwrite/chown). Staging in $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY
   269	            # is NOT enough — lnd owns that directory and can unlink our temp file
   270	            # and swap in a symlink before we write to it. Stage instead in a
   271	            # ROOT-ONLY directory (mode 0700, owned by root, that lnd cannot enter
   272	            # or replace) on the SAME tmpfs as /run/lnd, so the final move is a
   273	            # rename(2): atomic, and it replaces the destination without following
   274	            # a symlink lnd may have planted there.
   275	            staging=$(${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mktemp -d "/run/lnd-macaroons.XXXXXX")
   276	            trap '${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/rm -rf "$staging"' EXIT
   277	            ${lib.concatMapStrings (macaroon: ''
   278	              echo "Create custom macaroon ${macaroon}"
   279	              macaroonTmp="$staging/${macaroon}.macaroon"
   280	              # Read admin.macaroon AS the lnd user, never as root: it lives in
   281	              # lnd's own 0770 datadir, so a compromised lnd could plant a
   282	              # symlink there and have this root script dereference it to
   283	              # exfiltrate an arbitrary root-only file (backup passphrase, wg
   284	              # key, host keys) via the REST call below. Reading as the lnd user
   285	              # fails closed on such a symlink — lnd cannot read what lnd cannot
   286	              # read — and grants no access lnd does not already have. (The write
   287	              # path above is already symlink-hardened; this closes the read.)
   288	              adminMacaroonHex=$(${pkgs.util-linux}/bin/runuser -u ${cfg.user} -- ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -ps -u -c 99999 '${networkDir}/admin.macaroon')
   289	              # The response body is lnd-controlled. A macaroon is a few hundred
   290	              # bytes, so cap what this root script will buffer and write to the
   291	              # /run tmpfs at 64 KiB, and fail (jq -e) rather than write garbage
   292	              # when the reply carries no `.macaroon` (audit 2026-08-30, low).
   293	              ${curl} \
   294	                -H @<(printf 'Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: %s\n' "$adminMacaroonHex") \
   295	                -X POST \
   296	                -d '{"permissions":[${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.permissions}]}' \
   297	                ${restUrl}/macaroon |\
   298	                ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/head -c 65536 |\
   299	                ${pkgs.jq}/bin/jq -ce '.macaroon | strings' | ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -p -r > "$macaroonTmp"
   300	              chown ${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.user}: "$macaroonTmp"
   301	              # -T: the destination is a path lnd controls; without -T a
   302	              # symlink-to-directory planted there would make root move the
   303	              # file INTO that directory under the fixed basename instead of
   304	              # replacing the path (second opinion 2026-09-09).
   305	              ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mv -fT -- "$macaroonTmp" "$RUNTIME_DIRECTORY/${macaroon}.macaroon"
   306	            '') (attrNames cfg.macaroons)}
   307	          '';
   308	        in [
   309	          script
   310	        ];
   311	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.tor.enforce;
   312	    };
   313	
   314	    users.users.${cfg.user} = {
   315	      isSystemUser = true;
   316	      group = cfg.group;
   317	      extraGroups = [ "bitcoinrpc-public" ];
   318	      home = cfg.dataDir; # lnd creates .lnd dir in HOME
   319	    };
   320	    users.groups.${cfg.group} = {};
   321	    nix-bitcoin.operator = {
   322	      groups = [ cfg.group ];
   323	      allowRunAsUsers = [ cfg.user ];
   324	    };
   325

   327	      lnd-wallet-password.user = cfg.user;
   328	      lnd-key.user = cfg.user;
   329	      lnd-cert.user = cfg.user;
   330	      lnd-cert.permissions = "444"; # world readable
    60	    shebang ? "#!${pkgs.stdenv.shell} -e",
    61	    port,
    62	    authSecretPath,

    64	    onionService ? null,
    65	    certPath ? null

    33	      # @system-service whitelist and docker seccomp blacklist (except for "clone"
    34	      # which is a core requirement for systemd services)
    35	      # @system-service is defined in src/shared/seccomp-util.c (systemd source)
    36	      SystemCallFilter = [ "@system-service" "~add_key kcmp keyctl mbind move_pages name_to_handle_at personality process_vm_readv process_vm_writev request_key setns unshare userfaultfd" ];
    37	      SystemCallArchitectures = "native";
    38	  };
    39	
    40	  allowNetlink = {
    41	    RestrictAddressFamilies = self.defaultHardening.RestrictAddressFamilies + " AF_NETLINK";
    42	  };
    43	
    44	  nodejs = {
    45	    # Required for JIT compilation
    46	    MemoryDenyWriteExecute = false;
    47	    # Required by nodejs >= 18
    48	    SystemCallFilter = self.defaultHardening.SystemCallFilter ++ [ "@pkey" ];
    49	  };
    50	
    51	  # Allow takes precedence over Deny.
    52	  allowLocalIPAddresses = {
    53	    IPAddressAllow = [
    54	      "127.0.0.1/32"
    55	      "::1/128"
    56	      "169.254.0.0/16"
    57	    ];
    58	  };
    59	  allowAllIPAddresses = { IPAddressAllow = "any"; };
    60	  allowTor = self.allowLocalIPAddresses;
    61	  allowedIPAddresses = onlyLocal:
    62	    if onlyLocal
    63	    then self.allowLocalIPAddresses
    64	    else self.allowAllIPAddresses;

    71	    };

    75	      description = ''

    77	        from and to localhost and link-local addresses.
    78	      '';
    79	    };
    80	  };
    81

    84	    ${src}
    85	  '';
    86

    92	    version = 3;

    94	
    95	  # Convert a bind address, which may be a special INADDR_ANY address,
    96	  # to an actual IP address
    97	  address = addr:
    98	    if addr == "0.0.0.0" then
    99	      "127.0.0.1"
   100	    else if addr == "::" then
   101	      "::1"
   102	    else

   117	  };

   121	    readOnly = true;
   122	    inherit default;

     6	    enable = mkOption {
     7	      type = types.bool;
     8	      default = false;
     9	      description = ''
    10	        Whether to define a user named `operator` for convenient interactive access
    11	        to nix-bitcoin features (like `bitcoin-cli`).
    12	
    13	        When using nix-bitcoin as part of a larger system config, it makes sense
    14	        to set your main system user as the operator, by setting option
    15	        `nix-bitcoin.operator.name = "MAIN_USER_NAME";`.
    16	      '';
    17	    };
    18	    name = mkOption {
    19	      type = types.str;
    20	      default = "operator";
    21	      description = "Name of the operator user.";
    22	    };
    23	    groups = mkOption {
    24	      type = with types; listOf str;
    25	      default = [];
    26	      description = "Extra groups of the operatur user.";
    27	    };
    28	    allowRunAsUsers = mkOption {
    29	      type = with types; listOf str;
    30	      default = [];
    31	      description = "Users as which the operator is allowed to run commands.";
    32	    };
    33	  };
    34	
    35	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.operator;
    36	in {
    37	  inherit options;
    38	
    39	  config = mkIf cfg.enable {
    40	    users.users.${cfg.name} = {
    41	      isNormalUser = true;
    42	      extraGroups = [
    43	        "systemd-journal"
    44	        "proc" # Enable full /proc access and systemd-status
    45	      ] ++ cfg.groups;
    46	    };
    47	
    48	    security = mkIf (cfg.allowRunAsUsers != []) {
    49	      # Use doas instead of sudo if enabled
    50	      doas.extraConfig = mkIf config.security.doas.enable ''
    51	        ${lib.concatMapStrings (user: "permit nopass ${cfg.name} as ${user}\n") cfg.allowRunAsUsers}
    52	      '';
    53	      sudo.extraConfig = mkIf (!config.security.doas.enable) ''
    54	        ${cfg.name} ALL=(${builtins.concatStringsSep "," cfg.allowRunAsUsers}) NOPASSWD: ALL
    55	      '';
    56	    };
    57	  };

     4	  push:
     5	    # Code branches only. The `audits` branch carries generated LLM-audit
     6	    # transcripts, not code — running the VM suite on it would be pure waste.
     7	    # `auto/**` is the weekly fork-autotest pin candidate: its CANDIDATE READY

     9	    branches: [master, release, "auto/**"]

    11	  workflow_dispatch:
    12	
    13	env:
    14	  CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY: ${{ secrets.CACHIX_SIGNING_KEY }}
    15	  CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.CACHIX_AUTH_TOKEN }}
    16	
    17	# Strategy:
    18	# Job `build_test_drivers` evals all VM tests and builds all test drivers in a single Nix build.
    19	# Compared to launching a separate build job for each VM test, this avoids duplicated
    20	# builds of derivations that the test drivers have in common.
    21	#
    22	# The job matrix `test_scenario` runs the VM tests. Scenario jobs ALWAYS run —
    23	# build-to-cachix.sh early-exits green when a scenario's result is already in
    24	# the binary cache — so every push reports a real conclusion for all five
    25	# checks. (A job-level skip leaves the matrix contexts unreported, and the
    26	# release branch ruleset then blocks fast-forwards forever with
    27	# "required status checks are expected".)
    28	jobs:
    29	  build_test_drivers:
    30	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    31	    steps:
    32	      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
    33	      - uses: cachix/install-nix-action@b85815f71a6de0ddee80b8a80a98d43f4bcc66c7 # v31
    34	      - id: main
    35	        name: Build test drivers
    36	        run: |
    37	          echo "::group::Setup Nix env"
    38	          nix shell --inputs-from . nixpkgs#{bash,coreutils,cachix} -c ./test/ci/build_test_drivers.sh
    39	
    40	  test_scenario:
    41	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    42	    strategy:
    43	      fail-fast: false
    44	      matrix:
    45	        # `default` (secureNode) and `regtest` boot the full stack
    46	        # (bitcoind + lnd + electrs + btcpayserver/nbxplorer) and assert it
    47	        # works — this catches RPC-whitelist / service-health regressions.
    48	        # `netns` covers network-namespace isolation. `wireguard-lndconnect`
    49	        # boots the wireguard preset and reaches lnd REST through the tunnel
    50	        # from a second VM — the preset's firewall rules are its whole security
    51	        # model, so they are release-gated too (audit 2026-09-09).
    52	        scenario:
    53	          - default
    54	          - regtest
    55	          - netns
    56	          - wireguard-lndconnect
    57	    needs: build_test_drivers
    58	    steps:
    59	      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
    60	      # VM tests run QEMU inside the Nix build sandbox and need /dev/kvm
    61	      # accessible to the build users. GitHub runners have KVM hardware; open
    62	      # its permissions so the sandbox can use it.
    63	      - name: Enable KVM
    64	        run: |
    65	          echo 'KERNEL=="kvm", GROUP="kvm", MODE="0666", OPTIONS+="static_node=kvm"' | sudo tee /etc/udev/rules.d/99-kvm4all.rules
    66	          sudo udevadm control --reload-rules
    67	          sudo udevadm trigger --name-match=kvm
    68	      - uses: cachix/install-nix-action@b85815f71a6de0ddee80b8a80a98d43f4bcc66c7 # v31
    69	      - run: nix shell --inputs-from . nixpkgs#{bash,coreutils,cachix} -c ./test/ci/build.sh ${{ matrix.scenario }}
    70

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	
     3	# Modules integration test runner.
     4	# The tests (defined in ./tests.nix) use the NixOS testing framework and are executed in a VM.
     5	#
     6	# Usage:
     7	#   Run the basic set of tests. These are also run on the CI server.
     8	#   ./run-tests.sh
     9	#
    10	#   Test specific scenario
    11	#   ./run-tests.sh --scenario|-s <scenario>
    12	#
    13	#   - When <scenario> contains a space, <scenario> is treated as nix code defining
    14	#     a scenario. It is evaluated in the same context as other scenarios in ./tests.nix
    15	#
    16	#     Example:
    17	#     ./run-tests.sh -s "{ nix-bitcoin.nodeinfo.enable = true; }" container --run c nodeinfo
    18	#
    19	#   - When <scenario> does not name a scenario, the test is run with an adhoc scenario
    20	#     where services.<scenario> is enabled.
    21	#
    22	#     Example:
    23	#     ./run-tests.sh -s electrs
    24	#
    25	#   Run test(s) and link results to avoid garbage collection
    26	#   ./run-tests.sh [--scenario <scenario>] --out-link-prefix /tmp/nix-bitcoin-test
    27	#
    28	#   Pass extra args to nix-build
    29	#   ./run-tests.sh build --builders 'ssh://mybuildhost - - 15'
    30	#
    31	#   Run interactive test debugging
    32	#   ./run-tests.sh [--scenario <scenario>] debug
    33	#
    34	#     This starts the testing VM and drops you into a Python REPL where you can
    35	#     manually execute the tests from ./tests.py
    36	#
    37	#   Run a test scenario in a container
    38	#   sudo ./run-tests.sh [--scenario <scenario>] container
    39	#
    40	#     This is useful for quick experiments; containers start much faster than VMs.
    41	#     Running the Python test suite in containers is not yet supported.
    42	#     For now, creating NixOS containers requires root permissions.
    43	#     See ./lib/make-container.sh for a complete documentation.
    44	#
    45	#   Run a test scenario in a regular NixOS VM.
    46	#   No tests are executed, the machine's serial console is attached to your terminal.
    47	#   ./run-tests.sh [--scenario <scenario>] vm
    48	#
    49	#     This is useful for directly exploring a test configuration without the
    50	#     intermediate Python REPL layer.
    51	#     Run command 'q' inside the machine for instant poweroff.
    52	#
    53	#   Run tests from a snapshot copy of the source files
    54	#   ./run-tests.sh --copy-src|-c ...
    55	#
    56	#     This allows you to continue editing the nix-bitcoin sources while tests are running
    57	#     and reading source files.
    58	#     Files are copied to /tmp, a caching scheme helps minimizing copies.
    59	#
    60	#   Add custom scenarios from a file
    61	#   ./run-tests.sh --extra-scenarios ~/my/scenarios.nix ...
    62	
    63	set -eo pipefail
    64	
    65	scriptDir=$(cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}" && pwd)
    66	
    67	args=("$@")
    68	scenario=
    69	outLinkPrefix=
    70	scenarioOverridesFile=
    71	while :; do
    72	    case $1 in
    73	        --scenario|-s)
    74	            if [[ $2 ]]; then
    75	                scenario=$2
    76	                shift
    77	                shift
    78	            else
    79	                >&2 echo "Error: $1 requires an argument."
    80	                exit 1
    81	            fi
    82	            ;;
    83	        --out-link-prefix|-o)
    84	            if [[ $2 ]]; then
    85	                outLinkPrefix=$2
    86	                shift
    87	                shift
    88	            else
    89	                >&2 echo "Error: $1 requires an argument."
    90	                exit 1
    91	            fi
    92	            ;;
    93	        --extra-scenarios)
    94	            if [[ $2 ]]; then
    95	                scenarioOverridesFile=$2
    96	                shift
    97	                shift
    98	            else
    99	                >&2 echo "Error: $1 requires an argument."
   100	                exit 1
   101	            fi
   102	            ;;
   103	        --copy-src|-c)
   104	            shift
   105	            if [[ ! $_nixBitcoinInCopiedSrc ]]; then
   106	                . "$scriptDir/lib/copy-src.sh"
   107	                exit
   108	            fi
   109	            ;;
   110	        *)
   111	            break
   112	    esac
   113	done
   114	
   115	tmpDir=
   116	# Sets global var `tmpDir`
   117	makeTmpDir() {
   118	    if [[ ! $tmpDir ]]; then
   119	        tmpDir=$(mktemp -d /tmp/nix-bitcoin-tests.XXX)
   120	        # shellcheck disable=SC2064
   121	        trap "rm -rf '$tmpDir'" EXIT
   122	    fi
   123	}
   124	
   125	# Support explicit scenario definitions
   126	if [[ $scenario = *' '* ]]; then
   127	    makeTmpDir
   128	    scenarioOverridesFile=$tmpDir/scenario-overrides.nix
   129	    echo "{ scenarios, pkgs, lib, nix-bitcoin }: with lib; { tmp = $scenario; }" > "$scenarioOverridesFile"
   130	    scenario=tmp
   131	fi
   132	
   133	# Run the test. No temporary files are left on the host system.
   134	run() {
   135	    makeTmpDir
   136	    buildTestAttr .run --out-link "$tmpDir/run-vm"
   137	    NIX_BITCOIN_VM_DATADIR=$tmpDir "$tmpDir/run-vm/bin/run-vm" "$@"
   138	}
   139	
   140	debug() {
   141	    run --debug
   142	}
   143	
   144	container() {
   145	    local nixosContainer
   146	    if ! nixosContainer=$(type -p nixos-container) \
   147	       || grep -q '"/etc/nixos-containers"' "$nixosContainer"; then
   148	        local attr=container
   149	    else
   150	        # NixOS with `system.stateVersion` <22.05
   151	        local attr=containerLegacy
   152	    fi
   153	    echo "Building container"
   154	    makeTmpDir
   155	    export container=$tmpDir/container
   156	    buildTestAttr ".$attr" --out-link "$container"
   157	    export scriptDir
   158	    "$scriptDir/lib/make-container.sh" "$@"
   159	}
   160	
   161	# Run a regular NixOS VM
   162	vm() {
   163	    makeTmpDir
   164	    buildTestAttr .vm --out-link "$tmpDir/vm"
   165	    NIX_BITCOIN_VM_DATADIR=$tmpDir "$tmpDir/vm/bin/run-vm-in-tmpdir"
   166	}
   167	
   168	# Run the test by building the test derivation
   169	buildTest() {
   170	    buildTestAttr "" "$@"
   171	}
   172	
   173	evalTest() {
   174	    nixInstantiateTest "" "$@"
   175	    # Print out path
   176	    nix-store -q "$drv"
   177	    # Print drv path
   178	    realpath "$drv"
   179	}

     1	# Security-driven version overrides for packages whose pinned nixpkgs
     2	# version ships known vulnerabilities.
     3	#
     4	# Each override must be version-guarded so it turns inert once the pinned
     5	# nixpkgs catches up. Flake updates are then always safe to take.
     6	#
     7	# Example (from the clboss fund-theft fix, since resolved by
     8	# trimming clightning support from this fork):
     9	#
    10	#   clboss = if lagging pkgsUnstable.clboss "0.16.2" then pkgsUnstable.clboss.overrideAttrs (old: rec {
    11	#     version = "0.16.2";
    12	#     src = pkgs.fetchzip {
    13	#       url = "https://github.com/ZmnSCPxj/clboss/releases/download/v${version}/clboss-v${version}.tar.gz";
    14	#       hash = "...";
    15	#     };
    16	#   }) else pkgsUnstable.clboss;
    17	{ pkgs, pkgsUnstable }:
    18	let
    19	  inherit (pkgs) lib;
    20	  lagging = pkg: version: lib.versionOlder pkg.version version;
    21	in
    22	{
    23	  # 2.4.3 (2026-08-24) is a security release ("updating is recommended for
    24	  # servers shared with many users"); nixpkgs 26.05 carries 2.4.2. The nuget
    25	  # deps changed (HtmlSanitizer 9.1.982 -> 9.2.995 among others), so this is a
    26	  # full package copy with a regenerated lockfile (see ./btcpayserver/) —
    27	  # buildDotnetModule can't take nugetDeps via overrideAttrs. Drop the copy
    28	  # together with this override once the pin catches up.

     4	      url = "https://github.com/nixos/nixpkgs/archive/${rev}.tar.gz";
     5	      inherit sha256;
     6	    };
     7	
     8	  fetch = input: let

    10	  in fetchNixpkgs {

    38	    import base64
    39	    import urllib.parse as Url
    40	    from types import SimpleNamespace
    41	
    42	    def parse_lndconnect_url(url):
    43	        u = Url.urlparse(url)
    44	        data = {'host': u.hostname, 'port': u.port}
    45	        queries = Url.parse_qs(u.query)
    46	        macaroon = queries['macaroon'][0]
    47	        data['macaroon_hex'] = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(macaroon + '===').hex().upper()
    48	        return SimpleNamespace(**data)
    49	
    50	    client.start()
    51	    server.connect()
    52	
    53	    if not "is_interactive" in vars():
    54	
    55	      with subtest("connect client to server via WireGuard"):
    56	          server.wait_for_unit("wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0.service")
    57	
    58	          # Get WireGuard config from server and save it to `/tmp/wireguard.conf` on the client
    59	          wg_config = server.succeed("runuser -u operator -- nix-bitcoin-wg-connect server --text")
    60	          # Encode to base64
    61	          b64 = base64.b64encode(wg_config.encode('utf-8')).decode()
    62	          client.succeed(f"install -m 400 <(echo -n {b64} | base64 -d) /tmp/wireguard.conf")
    63	
    64	          # Connect to server via WireGuard
    65	          client.succeed("wg-quick up /tmp/wireguard.conf")
    66	
    67	          # Ping server from client
    68	          print(client.succeed("ping -c 1 -W 0.5 10.10.0.1"))
    69	
    70	      with subtest("lndconnect-wg"):
    71	          server.wait_for_unit("lnd.service")
    72	          lndconnect_url = server.succeed("runuser -u operator -- lndconnect-wg --url")
    73	          api = parse_lndconnect_url(lndconnect_url)
    74	          # Make lnd REST API call
    75	          client.succeed(
    76	              f"curl -fsS --max-time 3 --insecure --header 'Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: {api.macaroon_hex}' "
    77	              f"-X GET https://{api.host}:{api.port}/v1/getinfo"
    78	          )
    79	  '';

    70	There are two ways to establish a secure, direct connection:
    71	
    72	- Connecting via TLS. This requires installing your lightning app's
    73	  TLS Certificate on your mobile device.
    74	
    75	- Connecting via WireGuard. This approach is simpler and more versatile, and is
    76	  described in this guide.
    77	
    78	1. Install [Zeus](https://zeusln.app) (version ≥ 0.9.0) and
    79	   [WireGuard](https://www.wireguard.com/install/) on your mobile device.
    80	
    81	2. Add the following to your `configuration.nix`:
    82	   ```nix
    83	   imports = [
    84	     # Use this line when using the default deployment method
    85	     <nix-bitcoin/modules/presets/wireguard.nix>
    86	
    87	     # Use this line when using Flakes
    88	     (nix-bitcoin + /modules/presets/wireguard.nix)
    89	   ]
    90	
    91	   services.lnd.lndconnect.enable = true;
    92	   ```
    93	3. Deploy your configuration.
    94	
    95	4. If your node is behind an external firewall or NAT (e.g. a router), add the following port forwarding
    96	   rule to the external device:
    97	   - Port: 51820 (the default value of option `networking.wireguard.interfaces.wg-nb.listenPort`)
    98	   - Protocol: UDP
    99	   - Destination: IP of your node
   100	
   101	5. Setup WireGuard on your mobile device.
   102	
   103	   Run the following command on your node (as user `operator`) to create a QR code
   104	   for WireGuard:
   105	   ```bash
   106	   nix-bitcoin-wg-connect
   107	
   108	   # For debugging: Show the WireGuard config as text
   109	   nix-bitcoin-wg-connect --text
   110	   ```
   111	   The above commands automatically detect your node's external IP.\
   112	   To set a custom IP or hostname, run the following:
   113	   ```
   114	   nix-bitcoin-wg-connect 93.184.216.34
   115	   nix-bitcoin-wg-connect mynode.org
   116	   ```
   117	
   118	   Configure WireGuard:
   119	   - Press the `+` button in the bottom right corner
   120	   - Scan the QR code
   121	   - Add the tunnel
   122	
   123	6. Setup Zeus
   124	
   125	   Run the following command on your node (as user `operator`) to create a QR code for Zeus:
   126	
   127	   ```
   128	   lndconnect-wg
   129	   ```

from pathlib import Path
for p in Path('/nix/store').glob('*-source'):
    if not p.is_dir(): continue
    rev=p/'.git-revision'
    if rev.is_file():
        r=rev.read_text().strip()
        if r in ['b6c8664de9b6cc07fe5666a29f91884ba81197c4','78e9c786dc08cd4f3420c2395cd977206a9b1da2']:

                if f.exists(): print(sub, f.read_text()[:3500])
    for sub in ['BTCPayServer/BTCPayServer.csproj','config.go','go.mod']:

            if sub.startswith('BTCPay') or ('module github.com/lightningnetwork/lnd' in f.read_text()[:200] if sub=='go.mod' else False):

# Changelog

## 2.4.4

### Breaking changes

* **Checkout**: NFC payments are now disabled by default. Enable them under Store Settings > Checkout Experience. This change was introduced in 2.4.3 but was missing from its release notes. @rockstardev
* **Invoices**: Block zero-amount invoices by default. Stores can allow them in their settings (#7514) @NicolasDorier
* **Boltcards**: Remove the desktop smartcard setup and open the Boltcard app instead (#7515) @NicolasDorier
* **Store users**: Users must accept an invitation before joining a store (#7519) @dstrukt
* **Point of Sale**: Remove the per-request `notificationUrl`. Invoices now use the app's configured notification URL @Kukks
* **Server administration**: Remove legacy SSH settings and add deployment-provided `btcpay-host` integration, including `btcpay-host env` ([documentation](https://docs.btcpayserver.org/Development/HostIntegration/)) (#7511 #7543) @NicolasDorier
* **API Keys**: Use API key IDs instead of secrets for Greenfield API revocation (#7561) @NicolasDorier
* **Bitpay API**: Remove legacy BitPay Basic-auth API keys (#7561) @NicolasDorier

### New features

* **Stores**: Add invitations for store users (#7519) @dstrukt
* **Email Rules**: Add `StoreInvitePending` and `StoreUserJoined` triggers (#7519) @dstrukt

### Fixes

* **Crowdfund**: Keep the main image and last-updated date when contributions change (#7487) @inauman
* **Reports**: Display short values correctly and hide broken explorer links (#7506) @Psycarlo
* **Invoices**: Require permission to change an invoice's state (#7514) @NicolasDorier
* **Wallet**: Show an empty label instead of the wallet derivation scheme (#7521) @TChukwuleta
* **Plugins**: Show details and install actions for unlisted plugins found through search or direct links (#7524) @thgO-O
* **Point of Sale**: Fix checkout when products share the same title (#7540) @comwanga
* **Rates**: Replace the unavailable CryptoMarket provider with Notbank and update existing rate rules (#7526) @NicolasDorier
* **Rates**: Ignore invalid BTCTurk prices where the bid is higher than the ask @NicolasDorier
* **Payment Requests**: Do not add empty reference IDs to invoice searches @NicolasDorier
* Log users out immediately when they are demoted, disabled, unapproved, or have their email confirmation revoked (#7529) @NicolasDorier
* Keep monetization subscriptions from re-enabling accounts disabled by an administrator (#7523) @TChukwuleta
* **Greenfield**: Prevent restricted API keys from creating unrestricted keys (#7531) @NicolasDorier
* **Greenfield**: Prevent disabling the last active server administrator (#7541) @TChukwuleta
# Changelog

## 2.4.2

This release contains fix of a critical vulnerability that is being actively exploited. You need to update as fast as you can.
We recommend integrators to also update NBXplorer to version 2.6.10.

Those have been reported to us by @brunoerg and @benthecarman from the Bitcoin Red Team effort.

### Breaking change

* Greenfield: Disable Basic authentication by default five minutes after account creation, with opt-in available through account settings and the API (#7492) @NicolasDorier
# Changelog

## 2.4.3

This is a security release; updating is recommended for servers shared with many users.

## 2.4.2

This release contains fix of a critical vulnerability that is being actively exploited. You need to update as fast as you can.
We recommend integrators to also update NBXplorer to version 2.6.10.

Those have been reported to us by @brunoerg and @benthecarman from the Bitcoin Red Team effort.

/bin/bash -lc "python3 - <<'PY'
import sqlite3,base64,json
from pathlib import Path
con=sqlite3.connect('file:/nix/var/nix/db/db.sqlite?mode=ro',uri=True)
rows=con.execute(\"select path, hash from ValidPaths where path like '%-source'\").fetchall()
lock=json.loads(Path('flake.lock').read_text())
for key in ['nixpkgs','nixpkgs-unstable']:
    h=base64.b64decode(lock['nodes'][key]['locked']['narHash'].split('-',1)[1]).hex()
    for p,hs in rows:
        if hs=='sha256:'+h:
            print('MATCH',key,p)
            for rel in ['pkgs/by-name/ln/lnd/package.nix','pkgs/by-name/el/electrs/package.nix','pkgs/by-name/bt/btcpayserver/package.nix']:
                f=Path(p)/rel
                if f.is_file():
                    for i,s in enumerate(f.read_text().splitlines(),1):
                        if any(x in s for x in ['version =','pname =','tag =']): print(rel,i,s)
PY" in /home/dan/fork-audit/src-so-2026-10-01-114021

   789	        public static void AddRateProvider<T>(this IServiceCollection services) where T : class, IRateProvider
   790	        {
   791	            services.AddSingleton<IRateProvider, T>();
   792	        }
   793	
   794	        public static IServiceCollection AddBTCPayNetwork(this IServiceCollection services, BTCPayNetworkBase network)
   795	        {
   236	        type = lib.types.bool;
   237	        default = false;
   238	        description = ''
   239	          Enable filtering in IP forwarding.
   240	
   241	          This option only works with the nftables based firewall.
   242	        '';
   243	      };
   244	
   245	      connectionTrackingModules = lib.mkOption {
   246	        type = lib.types.listOf lib.types.str;
   247	        default = [ ];
   248	        example = [
   249	          "ftp"
   250	          "irc"
   251	          "sane"
   252	          "sip"
   253	          "tftp"
   254	          "amanda"
   255	          "h323"
   256	          "netbios_sn"
   257	          "pptp"
   258	          "snmp"
   259	        ];
   260	        description = ''
   261	          List of connection-tracking helpers that are auto-loaded.
   262	          The complete list of possible values is given in the example.
   263	
   264	          As helpers can pose as a security risk, it is advised to
   265	          set this to an empty list and disable the setting
   266	          networking.firewall.autoLoadConntrackHelpers unless you
   267	          know what you are doing. Connection tracking is disabled
   268	          by default.
   269	
   270	          Loading of helpers is recommended to be done through the
   271	          CT target.  More info:
   272	          <https://home.regit.org/netfilter-en/secure-use-of-helpers/>
   273	        '';
   274	      };
   275	
   276	      autoLoadConntrackHelpers = lib.mkOption {
   277	        type = lib.types.bool;
   278	        default = false;
   279	        description = ''
   280	          Whether to auto-load connection-tracking helpers.
   281	          See the description at networking.firewall.connectionTrackingModules
   282	
   283	          (needs kernel 3.5+)
   284	        '';
   285	      };
   286	
   287	      extraPackages = lib.mkOption {
   288	        type = lib.types.listOf lib.types.package;
   289	        default = [ ];
   290	        example = lib.literalExpression "[ pkgs.ipset ]";
   291	        description = ''
   292	          Additional packages to be included in the environment of the system
   293	          as well as the path of networking.firewall.extraCommands.
   294	        '';
   295	      };
   296	
   297	      interfaces = lib.mkOption {
   298	        default = { };
   299	        type = with lib.types; attrsOf (submodule [ { options = commonOptions; } ]);
   300	        description = ''
   301	          Interface-specific open ports.
   302	        '';
   303	      };
   304	
   305	      allInterfaces = lib.mkOption {
   306	        internal = true;
   307	        visible = false;
   308	        default = {
   309	          default = lib.mapAttrs (name: value: cfg.${name}) commonOptions;
   310	        }
   311	        // cfg.interfaces;
   312	        type = with lib.types; attrsOf (submodule [ { options = commonOptions; } ]);
   313	        description = ''
   314	          All open ports.
   315	        '';
   316	      };
   317	    }
   318	    // commonOptions;
   319	  };
   320	
   321	  config = lib.mkIf cfg.enable {
   322	    assertions = [
   323	      {
   324	        assertion = cfg.filterForward -> config.networking.nftables.enable;
   325	        message = "filterForward only works with the nftables based firewall";
   326	      }
   327	      {
   328	        assertion =
   329	          cfg.autoLoadConntrackHelpers -> lib.versionOlder config.boot.kernelPackages.kernel.version "6";
   330	        message = "conntrack helper autoloading has been removed from kernel 6.0 and newer";
   331	      }
   332	    ];
   333	
   334	    networking.firewall.trustedInterfaces = [ "lo" ];
   335	
   336	    environment.systemPackages = [ cfg.package ] ++ cfg.extraPackages;
   337	
   338	    boot.kernelModules =
   339	      (lib.optional cfg.autoLoadConntrackHelpers "nf_conntrack")
   340	      ++ map (x: "nf_conntrack_${x}") cfg.connectionTrackingModules;
   341	    boot.extraModprobeConfig = lib.optionalString cfg.autoLoadConntrackHelpers ''
   342	      options nf_conntrack nf_conntrack_helper=1
   343	    '';
   344	  };
   345	}

    91	            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(inner);

    95	        public BackgroundFetcherState GetState()
    96	        {
    97	            var state = new BackgroundFetcherState()
    98	            {
    99	                LastRequested = LastRequested
   100	            };
   101	            if (_Latest is LatestFetch fetch && fetch.Latest is not null)

   114	        public void LoadState(BackgroundFetcherState state)
   115	        {
   116	            if (state.LastRequested is DateTimeOffset)
   117	                this.LastRequested = state.LastRequested;
   118	            if (state.LastUpdated is DateTimeOffset updated && state.Rates is List<BackgroundFetcherRate> rates)

   122	                    Latest = rates.Select(r => new PairRate(r.Pair, r.BidAsk)).ToArray(),

   124	                    NextRefresh = updated + RefreshRate,
   125	                    Expiration = updated + ValidatyTime
   126	                };
   127	                _Latest = fetch;
   128	            }
   129	        }

   131	        TimeSpan _RefreshRate = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(30);
   132	        /// <summary>
   133	        /// The timespan after which <see cref="UpdateIfNecessary(CancellationToken)"/> will get the rates from the underlying rate provider
   134	        /// </summary>

   161	            set
   162	            {
   163	                var diff = value - _ValidatyTime;
   164	                var latest = _Latest;
   165	                if (latest != null)
   166	                    latest.Expiration += diff;
   167	                _ValidatyTime = value;
   168	            }
   169	        }
   170	
   171	        public DateTimeOffset NextUpdate
   172	        {
   173	            get
   174	            {
   175	                var latest = _Latest;
   176	                if (latest == null)
   177	                    return DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
   178	                return latest.NextRefresh;
   179	            }
   180	        }
   181	
   182	        public bool DoNotAutoFetchIfExpired { get; set; }
   183	        readonly static TimeSpan MaxBackoff = TimeSpan.FromMinutes(5.0);
   184	
   185	        public async Task<LatestFetch> UpdateIfNecessary(CancellationToken cancellationToken)
   186	        {
   187	            if (NextUpdate <= DateTimeOffset.UtcNow)
   188	            {
   189	                try
   190	                {
   191	                    await Fetch(_Latest?.Context, cancellationToken);
   192	                }
   193	                catch { } // Exception is inside _Latest
   194	                return _Latest;
   195	            }
   196	            return _Latest;
   197	        }
   198	
   199	        LatestFetch _Latest;
   200	        public Task<PairRate[]> GetRatesAsync(IRateContext context, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
   201	        {
   202	            return GetRatesAsyncCore(context, cancellationToken);
   203	        }
   204	        async Task<PairRate[]> GetRatesAsyncCore(IRateContext context, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
   205	        {
   206	            LastRequested = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
   207	            var latest = _Latest;
   208	            if (!DoNotAutoFetchIfExpired && latest != null && latest.Expiration <= DateTimeOffset.UtcNow + TimeSpan.FromSeconds(1.0))
   209	            {
   210	                latest = null;
   211	            }
   212	            return (latest ?? (await Fetch(context, cancellationToken))).GetResult();
   213	        }
   214	        public Task<PairRate[]> GetRatesAsync(CancellationToken cancellationToken)
   215	        {
   216	            return GetRatesAsyncCore(null, cancellationToken);
   217	        }
   218	
   219	        /// <summary>
   220	        /// The last time this rate provider has been used
   221	        /// </summary>
   222	        public DateTimeOffset? LastRequested { get; set; }
   223	
   224	        public DateTimeOffset? Expiration
   225	        {
   226	            get
   227	            {
   228	                if (_Latest is LatestFetch f)
   229	                {
   230	                    return f.Expiration;
   231	                }
   232	                return null;
   233	            }
   234	        }
   235	
   236	        public RateSourceInfo RateSourceInfo => _Inner.RateSourceInfo;
   237	
   238	        private async Task<LatestFetch> Fetch(IRateContext context, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
   239	        {
   240	            cancellationToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
   241	            var previous = _Latest;
   242	            var fetch = new LatestFetch();
   243	            try
   244	            {
   245	                var rates = await _Inner.GetRatesAsyncWithMaybeContext(context, cancellationToken);
   246	                fetch.Latest = rates;
   247	                fetch.Context = context;
   248	                fetch.Updated = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
   249	                fetch.Expiration = fetch.Updated + ValidatyTime;
   250	                fetch.NextRefresh = fetch.Updated + RefreshRate;
   251	            }
   252	            catch (Exception ex)
   253	            {
   254	                if (previous != null)
   255	                {
   256	                    fetch.Latest = previous.Latest;
   257	                    fetch.Expiration = previous.Expiration;
   258	                    fetch.Backoff = previous.Backoff * 2;
   259	                    if (fetch.Backoff > MaxBackoff)
   260	                        fetch.Backoff = MaxBackoff;
   261	                }
   262	                else
   263	                {
   264	                    fetch.Expiration = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
   265	                }

    14	        class WrapperRateProvider
    15	        {
    16	            private readonly IRateProvider _inner;
    17	            public Exception? Exception { get; private set; }
    18	            public TimeSpan Latency { get; set; }
    19	            public WrapperRateProvider(IRateProvider inner)
    20	            {
    21	                _inner = inner;
    22	            }
    23	            public async Task<PairRate[]> GetRatesAsync(IRateContext? context, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    24	            {
    25	                DateTimeOffset now = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
    26	                try
    27	                {
    28						return await _inner.GetRatesAsyncWithMaybeContext(context, cancellationToken);
    29	                }
    30	                catch (Exception ex)
    31	                {
    32	                    Exception = ex;
    33	                    return Array.Empty<PairRate>();
    34	                }
    35	                finally
    36	                {
    37	                    Latency = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow - now;
    38	                }
    39	            }
    40	        }
    41	        public class QueryRateResult
    42	        {
    43	            public QueryRateResult(string exchangeName, TimeSpan latency, PairRate[] pairRates)
    44	            {
    45	                Exchange = exchangeName;
    46	                Latency = latency;
    47	                PairRates = pairRates;
    48	            }
    49	
    50	            public TimeSpan Latency { get; set; }
    51	            public PairRate[] PairRates { get; set; }

    53	            public string Exchange { get; internal set; }
    54	        }
    55	        public RateProviderFactory(IHttpClientFactory httpClientFactory,IEnumerable<IRateProvider> rateProviders)
    56	        {
    57	            _httpClientFactory = httpClientFactory;
    58	            foreach (var prov in rateProviders)
    59	            {
    60	                Providers.Add(prov.RateSourceInfo.Id ?? "", prov);
    61	            }
    62	            InitExchanges();
    63	        }
    64	        private readonly IHttpClientFactory _httpClientFactory;
    65	        public Dictionary<string, IRateProvider> Providers { get; } = new Dictionary<string, IRateProvider>();
    66	        void InitExchanges()
    67	        {
    68	            foreach (var provider in Providers.ToArray())
    69	            {
    70	                var prov = Providers[provider.Key];
    71	                if (prov is IContextualRateProvider)
    72	                {
    73	                    Providers[provider.Key] = prov;
    74	                }
    75	                else
    76	                {
    77	                    var prov2 = new BackgroundFetcherRateProvider(prov);
    78	                    prov2.RefreshRate = TimeSpan.FromMinutes(1.0);
    79	                    prov2.ValidatyTime = TimeSpan.FromMinutes(5.0);
    80	                    Providers[provider.Key] = prov2;
    81	                }
    82	                var rsi = provider.Value.RateSourceInfo;
    83	                AvailableRateProviders.Add(new(rsi.Id, rsi.DisplayName, rsi.Url));
    84	            }
    85	
    86	            AvailableRateProviders.Sort((a, b) => StringComparer.Ordinal.Compare(a.DisplayName, b.DisplayName));
    87	        }
    88	
    89	        public List<RateSourceInfo> AvailableRateProviders { get; } = new List<RateSourceInfo>();
    90	
    91	        public async Task<QueryRateResult> QueryRates(string exchangeName, IRateContext? context = null, CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)
    92	        {
    93	            Providers.TryGetValue(exchangeName, out var directProvider);
    94	            directProvider ??= NullRateProvider.Instance;
    95	
    96	            var wrapper = new WrapperRateProvider(directProvider);
    97	            var value = await wrapper.GetRatesAsync(context, cancellationToken);
    98	            return new QueryRateResult(exchangeName, wrapper.Latency, value)
    99	            {
   100	                Exception = wrapper.Exception != null ? new ExchangeException() { Exception = wrapper.Exception, ExchangeName = exchangeName } : null
   101	            };
   102	        }
   103	    }

pos=len(b'openssh-key-v1\\0')
def field():

cipher=field();kdf=field();opts=field()
